Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 13
--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes to remove such that the two black nodes (N and G) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the connecting paths between them.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:** N and G.
2. **Identify the immediate neighbors of N:** A, B, C, and O.
3. **Identify the immediate neighbors of G:** I and B.
4. **Trace all paths from N to G:**

   - Path 1: N -> B -> G
   - Path 2: N -> B -> R -> J -> H -> I -> G
   - Path 3: N -> B -> R -> Q -> I -> G
   - Path 4: N -> C -> H -> I -> G

5. **Evaluate the bottlenecks in the paths:**

   - All paths from N to G must go through node B or connect via intermediates to node I.
   - Specifically, N can reach G via B directly, or via intermediate nodes like C and H.

6. **Identify the minimal cut set:**

   - If we remove B and I, all paths from N to G are disconnected.
   - Removing any one of the nodes B or I individually will not suffice because other paths will still exist.

7. **Check minimal removal:**

   - Removing both B and I completely disconnects the black nodes N and G.

Thus, the minimum number of nodes to remove to disconnect the two black nodes is:

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, I]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': ['B', 'C']}
